## D627 Heap-profile sampling is off unless the process asks for a heap profile

Summary: PiG does not sample heap allocations for profiling unless you set PIG_PROFILE to heap or allocs or set GODEBUG=memprofilerate; that saves about a megabyte of resident memory.

Audience: runtime

Stock disposition: inert capability. It selects no product behavior and changes no output; `PIG_PROFILE=heap` or `allocs`, or a `GODEBUG` with `memprofilerate`, turns it off.

What: the Go runtime samples one allocation in every 512 KiB (`runtime.MemProfileRate`) and keeps a stack record, a special on the object and a slot in a hash table for each sample. The init of `internal/memprofrate`, which `coding/cli` imports, sets `runtime.MemProfileRate` to 0 unless `PIG_PROFILE` names `heap` or `allocs`, or the runtime already holds a rate other than its default (the user's `GODEBUG=memprofilerate=N`, parsed before any init). The package imports only `runtime`, `strings` and `syscall`, so its init is among the first of the process's package inits. The rate cannot be set before the runtime's own first allocations (`//go:debug` rejects `memprofilerate`), so the runtime takes one more sample on each P's next allocation after the change; that sample allocates the 1,439,992-byte hash table, and no sample follows it. The profiling-buckets memory class (`/memory/classes/profiling/buckets:bytes`) therefore still reads 1.457 MB (300 of 300 runs of `pig --version` at this change); it is address space whose pages stay untouched, and the gain below is the resident part, the pages that a default run's samples touch.

What a user loses: nothing unless they profile the heap. `PIG_PROFILE=heap|allocs` and `GODEBUG=memprofilerate=N` keep sampling on from the start of the process; a heap profile that another tool takes from a default run is empty.

Scope: every `pig` process (interactive, print, json, rpc, `--listen`) and every Piglet Binary, which is built from `cmd/pig`, carry the init. A Go extension subprocess built on the pinned extension SDK, and the Node processes of TypeScript extensions, do not import it and keep the runtime default; that is scope, not a gap this record closes.

Pi source: none. Node has no sampling heap profiler running by default.

Why: measured paired and interleaved, same harness invocation, pinned xd6702 node1 cores, base 1accad07e9dc against this change, the same base binary twice as the floor (n = 12 interleaved rounds on clean benchmark cores): interactive scenario peak own memory -1.5 MB (11 of 12), 40-prompt session own memory -1.05 MB (9 of 10) and resident memory -1.75 MB, 20-prompt session resident memory -0.9 MB (12 of 12), `-p` headless user instructions -0.9 % (12 of 12), each against a same-binary floor; measured on the branch before the merge onto the current line, by the lane that built it. The same binary with `GODEBUG=memprofilerate=0` from the first instruction measured the same rows within the floor. No CPU time, wall time or time-to-interactive claim: those did not move beyond the floor (time to interactive +0.15 ms in 28 pairs, floor -0.6 ms).

Call-site markers: `internal/memprofrate/memprofrate.go` (`init`), `coding/cli/main.go` (the import).

Locked by: `TestSamplingFollowsTheRequest` (`internal/memprofrate`: child processes on one P with no request, a CPU or metrics profile, `heap`, `allocs` and `GODEBUG=memprofilerate=65536`; the rate, whether 64 MiB of allocations from one function leave records, and that the profiling-buckets class does not grow during them), `TestRateFor`, `TestCommandProcessSamplesNoAllocations` (`coding/cli`: the command's process runs at rate 0).

Remove when: the Go runtime stops allocating the profile table and touching its pages for a program that never reads the profile, or a measurement shows the sampling costs no resident memory.

Approval: owner, 2026-11-12T20:58Z, through the maintainer ("D627 APPROVED"), after being told that the runtime samples heap allocations in every normal run, that nothing in PiG reads the samples unless a profile is asked for, that D627 turns the sampling off by default, and that a user who wants a heap profile must ask for it.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern
SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:7f7a5c95060b66c0

## D653 A fused Piglet member's CLI hook owns the command line, a print run's output and exit status, and the default retry config

Summary: A Piglet Binary with a fused Go member can run code before PiG parses its arguments, to serve the invocation or rewrite it, to own a print run's output and exit status, and to set the retry defaults.

Audience: author

Stock disposition: inert capability. Stock PiG links no member, so no hook is registered, `clihook.Registered` returns nil and every path below it is the path of Pi. A fused member selects the seam by calling `clihook.Register` from its `init`; nothing else turns it on.

What: the package `github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/extensions/sdk/clihook` exports `Register`, the types `Hook`, `Env`, `Result` and `RetryPolicy`, and `Stdout` and `SetExitCode` for the member's extension. `Registered`, `SetStdout` and `ExitCode` are PiG's own wiring and are documented as such: `coding/cli` is another package, so they cannot be unexported, and no member calls them. A second `Register` replaces the first, and `Register(nil)` removes the hook.

`Main` calls the hook once, after the Piglet Binary is verified and its strip is recorded, and before any command dispatch (the auth, login, package and `--version` or `--help` handling all come after it). The hook receives `Env`: the arguments (`os.Args[1:]`), stdin, stdout and stderr, `Getenv`, `Chdir`, and whether stdin and stdout are terminals. It returns `Result`:

- `Handled`: the hook served the invocation (its own help, version or subcommands, a protocol on stdio). PiG parses nothing and exits through `exitProcess` with `Exit`, so the usual shutdown runs.
- `Args`: what PiG parses when the hook did not serve the invocation. A nil `Args` is an empty command line, not "unchanged": a hook that wants no rewrite returns `Env.Args`.
- `Retry`: the retry defaults of a session run in place of the built-in 3 retries, 2000 ms first delay and 60000 ms cap (`RetryPolicy`; the retry stays enabled). A `retry` member of settings.json still overrides each field it names, as it overrides the built-in value.
- `OwnPrintOutput`: the run is a print run whose stdout the member's extension writes itself. PiG's print mode then hands the member the raw stdout writer (`Stdout`, nil in every other run), writes nothing to stdout in text or JSON mode, and does not write the stderr line that a failed text run ends with (the error message of the last assistant message), because the member words that itself. The run's status stays PiG's: 1 for a failed run, whatever the member recorded.
- `SetExitCode`: the status the process exits with after a print run that succeeded in PiG's terms, when it is not 0. A recorded 0 changes nothing.

Scope: Go only, in the host. The seam is not on the extension wire (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/protocol.go` is untouched) and not on `sdk.Context`: a fused member runs in the host's process before any extension process exists, like the D91 frontend member. A fused member is Go by construction, so the seam is Go only by necessity, not by choice. So the rule that a capability lands in every SDK does not apply, and no conformance row exists.

Pi source: none. Pi's `main.ts` parses `process.argv` itself and has no member that may serve, rewrite or replace the command line, own a print run's output or status, or set the retry defaults. The built-in defaults the hook replaces stay as `builtinRetryConfig` records them (`settings-manager.ts:21-22`).

Why a Piglet cannot supply it itself: the hook needs `os.Args` before PiG parses them, and a fused extension runs after parsing; it may not write `os.Stdout` (PiG's print mode takes it over and sends stray writes to stderr), call `os.Exit` or `os.Chdir`, and PiG's print mode decides the status and the stderr line of a failed run. A Piglet that ships its own command line (its own flags, help and version, a protocol on stdio, its own output format and status codes) can do it in no other place.

Stock behavior: with no hook registered, `Main`, `runPrintModeIn` and `GetRetrySettings` do what they did: the same arguments, the same stdout, the same stderr line, the same status, and the retry defaults 3 / 2000 / 60000. `TestClihookOnTheProductionPath` compares a run through a hook that changes nothing with a run without one, for a success and a failure in text and JSON mode; the parity families `print` and `json` pass against the Pi oracle.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/main.go` (the hook call and the retry defaults it sets in `Main`, the `clihook.ExitCode` read after a print run), `coding/cli/print_mode.go` (`runPrintModeIn`: the stdout handed to the member, and the stderr of the result line), `internal/codingagent/settings.go` (`GetRetrySettings`, `SetDefaultRetryConfig`, `DefaultRetryConfig`) and `extensions/sdk/clihook/clihook.go` (the contract).

Locked by: `TestClihookOnTheProductionPath` (`coding/cli/clihook_test.go`: a child process that registered the hook runs the real command line; rows for the baseline, a pass-through hook, serving the invocation, rewriting the command line, owning the print output in text and JSON mode, the exit status, a failed run's stderr line and the retry policy), `TestRegisteredIsNilWithoutAHook`, `TestRegisterReplacesThePreviousHook`, `TestHookSeesTheEnvAndReturnsItsResult`, `TestStdoutIsNilUntilPrintModeSetsIt`, `TestExitCodeRecordsTheLastStatus` and `TestStateIsSafeAcrossGoroutines` (`extensions/sdk/clihook`), `TestRetrySettingsWithoutASetterAreUpstreamsDefaults` and `TestDefaultRetryConfigIsReplaceable` (`internal/codingagent`).

Remove when: Pi accepts a hook of its own for the command line, a print run's output and status, and the retry defaults, or no Piglet fuses a member that registers one. Then the package, the three call sites, `SetDefaultRetryConfig` and this record go.

Approval: owner, 2026-11-15T06:56:17Z. Owner decision: approved 2026-11-15T06:56:17Z (handoffs/0.8.0/OWNER-DECISIONS.md, that line): D653 and the stacked retry-decider seam (D654) are approved for 0.8.0.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern
SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:3870986246d2c356

## D654 A fused member decides which failed model requests PiG retries, how long it waits, and whether PiG prints its retry lines (`clihook.RetryPolicy.Decide`, `Silent`, `HTTPIdleTimeoutMs`)

Summary: A fused Piglet member decides per failed model request whether PiG retries and after how long, can silence PiG's retry lines, and can set the default HTTP idle time-out.

Audience: author

Stock disposition: inert capability. With no fused member that names a decider, `Silent` or `HTTPIdleTimeoutMs`, PiG retries by Pi's rule, prints Pi's retry lines and keeps its 300 s idle time-out. A failed request records its status and headers only when a decider is installed (the session marks the request's context with `ai.WithFailureResponse`), so a stock failed request allocates what it allocated before: `TestFailedRequestAllocationsWithoutDeciderMatchTheLineBeforeTheSeam`.

What: `clihook.Result.Retry` takes a `Decide func(RetryFailure) RetryDecision`. For each failed model request of a session run, and for each failed summarization (compaction) request, PiG calls it with the attempt, the retry budget (`settings.json` `retry.maxRetries`), the error text, the HTTP status (0 when no response arrived), the lower-cased response headers and whether the stream had emitted an event before it failed. The answer is `Retry` and `DelayMs`. A refusal ends the run with the failure as Pi's own rule does for an error it does not retry. `Silent` removes the interactive status line and the final "Retry failed after ..." line, for a member that renders its own; Escape still cancels the retry delay in both modes. `HTTPIdleTimeoutMs` replaces the default HTTP idle time-out when `settings.json` names none. A `message_end` replacement from an extension keeps the failure response of the message it replaces unless it names its own. The failure response is in-process only: it is not written to a session file, to RPC or to JSON mode (`TestAssistantMessageFailureResponseIsNotSerialized`).

Pi source: Pi decides by `isRetryableAssistantError` (`.upstream/current/packages/ai/src/utils/retry.ts:273`, called at `retry.ts:238` and `agent-session.ts:3871`) and waits `baseDelayMs * 2^(attempt-1)` (`retry.ts:149`, `agent-session.ts:3951`). That stays the default. During the wait Pi's interactive mode swaps in an Escape handler that cancels it. The 300 s idle time-out is `DEFAULT_HTTP_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` (`settings-manager.ts:929`).

Why: a Piglet cannot supply it. The retry rule and the delay run inside the session on the request path, where an extension sees only the final failed message; it has no status, no headers and no stream state, and cannot refuse or schedule a retry. A product that follows a provider's `retry-after` or treats a cut stream differently from a refused request needs the decision in the host. The retry lines are PiG's own terminal output, which an extension may not write over or remove.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/settings.go` (`SetDefaultRetryDecider`), `coding/session.go` (`willRetryAfterAgentEnd`, `summarizationRetryOptions`), `coding/session_recovery.go` (`prepareRetry`, the delay), `coding/session_cache_warming.go` (the request marker), `ai/assistant_retry.go`, `ai/stream_builder.go` (`recordFailureResponse`), `internal/codingagent/interactive_events.go` (the silent retry), `agent/message_json.go` (`ReplaceMessageInPlace`), `coding/cli/main.go` (the hook wiring).

Locked by: `TestFailedRequestAllocationsWithoutDeciderMatchTheLineBeforeTheSeam`, `TestFailedRequestWithoutRecordFailureResponseKeepsNoFailureResponse`, `TestFailedRequestRecordsFailureResponseOnEveryHTTPPath`, `TestFailedRequestRecordsStreamStartedAfterACutStream`, `TestFailureResponseDoesNotSerialize` (`ai/failure_response_test.go`); `TestAssistantMessageFailureResponseIsNotSerialized`, `TestReplaceMessageInPlaceKeepsTheFailureResponse` (`agent/failure_response_test.go`); `TestDeciderSeesTheFailedResponseOfARealProvider`, `TestDeciderSeesAStreamThatWasCutAfterItStarted`, `TestStockSessionKeepsNoFailureResponseOnAFailedMessage`, `TestSummarizationRetriesPerTheInstalledDecider` (`coding/session_failure_response_test.go`); `TestSilentRetryKeepsTheEscapeHandlerThatCancelsTheDelay` (`internal/codingagent/interactive_retry_silent_test.go`); `TestPrintModeRetriesPerTheInstalledDeciderAndWritesNoRetryLine`, `TestPrintModeStopsWhenTheDeciderRefuses`, `TestHTTPIdleTimeoutOverrideReachesTheDispatcher`, `TestMainAppliesTheHooksRetryPolicyAndIdleTimeout` (`coding/cli/retry_decider_hook_test.go`).

Evidence: the allocation test fails when a failed request builds its failure response without a decider; the Escape test fails on the first form of the Silent break (it left the Escape handler unset); the per-provider test fails when a provider's header site does not note the response. Bedrock and the other providers have no header site: they report no HTTP response headers through `ProviderRequestError`, so their failure response carries the status only.

Remove when: Pi gives an extension a retry decision hook per failed request, or the Piglet needing it no longer exists.

Approval: owner, 2026-11-15T06:56:17Z. Owner decision: approved 2026-11-15T06:56:17Z (handoffs/0.8.0/OWNER-DECISIONS.md, that line): D653 and the stacked retry-decider seam (D654) are approved for 0.8.0.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

## D655 A Go program can read a branch's entries by reference and read only the bodies it needs (`BranchRefs`, `ReadEntries`)

Summary: A program that embeds PiG can list a branch's entries without reading them and then read, with an error and a context, only the entries it picks, so a page of a long session costs the page.

Audience: author

Stock disposition: inert capability. Stock PiG selects nothing: no command, extension host, RPC method or default reads a branch this way. The program that embeds `coding.SessionManager` calls the two methods when it wants them; the extension SDKs and the extension host's `getBranch` are unchanged and stay Pi-shaped.

What: Pi holds every entry decoded in memory (`SessionManager.getBranch`, `getEntry`), so it has no selective read and no read that can fail after the file is opened. PiG keeps the entries of a long session in its snapshot of the file and reads them back on demand (D112), so `GetBranch` decodes every message of the branch before it returns (2000 entries of 4 KiB: 1744 record reads and decodes for a page of 5), `GetEntry` reads one entry with no batch, and none of the getters has an error or a context. Two methods of the session manager and one value type add what an embedding program needs, and change no existing method, the eager getters, the session file or the wire:

- `BranchRef` (`coding.BranchRef`): `ID`, `ParentID`, `Type`, `Role` (the message's role, empty for any other entry) and `Timestamp` of one entry. It is a plain value: no file handle, no part of the snapshot. The log is append-only, so an id names the same entry for the life of the session; a leaf move changes what `BranchRefs` returns and never what `ReadEntries` returns for an id.
- `BranchRefs(fromID...)`: `GetBranch`'s path as references: the same entries in the same order, the same rule for `fromID` (the current leaf when none is given; an unknown id or an empty one gives nil), and an id that two entries carry resolved as `GetBranch` resolves it (the newest). It reads no entry body.
- `ReadEntries(ctx, ids)`: the entries with the given ids, decoded, in the order given (an id given twice is returned twice), read in one batch in file order. The session's lock is held only to find the entries, so an append does not wait for the read. An unknown id, a record that cannot be read back as it was indexed (the error is a `*coding.RecordChangedError`, which `errors.As` finds, and it names the entry), and a context that is done before the read or between the records of two sources (each compressed block of a long session is one) return an error and no entries: no partial result, and no panic. A done context's error is the context's. `EntryReads` counts the records the call read.

Why: an embedding program that shows a page of a long session needs the order and the control records of the whole branch and the bodies of one page; reading the branch whole is the cost it cannot avoid today, and the internal lazy entry that would let it read less panics when its record cannot be read. The request is issue #451; the design is the proposal's section 5 (owner decision 11, 0.8.0).

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/session_branch_refs.go` (`BranchRef`, `BranchRefs`, `ReadEntries`), `internal/sessionentry/lazy.go` (`MaterializeAllContext`).

Locked by: `TestBranchRefsAreGetBranch`, `TestBranchRefsReadNoBody`, `TestBranchRefsCostNoReads`, `TestReadEntriesKeepsTheOrderAndReadsOnlyTheGivenEntries`, `TestReadEntriesOfAnUnknownIDIsAnErrorAndNoPartialResult`, `TestReadEntriesOfAChangedRecordIsTheRecordChangedError`, `TestReadEntriesOfACancelledContextReadsNothing`, `TestReadEntriesDuringAppends` and `TestReadEntriesOfASessionWithoutASnapshot` (`internal/codingagent/session_branch_refs_test.go`), `TestMaterializeAllContextStopsBetweenSources` (`internal/sessionentry/lazy_context_test.go`), `ExampleSessionManager_ReadEntries` (`coding/session_branch_reads_example_test.go`); measured by `BenchmarkBranchRefsOf10000` and `BenchmarkReadEntries50Of10000`.

Remove when: Pi has a selective branch read of its own, or the embedding programs that use it drop it.

Approval: owner decision 11 for 0.8.0 (issue #451, additive Go-embedding read API, errors not panics, reference lifetime as specified), relayed by the release coordinator on 2026-11-16.
SCRUTINIZED:approved
